#2929. History Says Investing $200 a Month in This Vanguard ETF Could Build Life-Changing Wealth Without a Single Stock Pick

Worried about trying to pick stocks during a stock market crash? No problem. VOO has you covered.

10 stocks we like better than Vanguard S&P 500 ETF ›

The recent sell-off in artificial intelligence (AI) stocks triggered a correction in the stock market. The S&P 500 index (^GSPC -0.25%) fell by more than 10% from its recent highs and is struggling to remain in positive territory for the year.

During times like these, it's easy for investors to worry that they're picking the wrong stocks. Or worry that the stock market is a bad place to put their money -- what if the S&P 500 goes down by another 10% next month?

If you want to invest in the stock market, times like these are actually some of the best moments to put money to work, because many stocks are trading at lower prices than just a few weeks ago.

And you don't have to pick stocks to make great money in the stock market. Just buying a low-cost index fund like the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO -0.28%) can deliver life-changing returns in the long run.

Let's look at just how much you could make from steady $200 monthly investments in VOO.

The Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO) is one of the most popular ways to invest in the stock market because it charges ultra-low fees (0.03% expense ratio) and it holds 500 of the largest, most important publicly traded stocks in America. When you buy VOO, you're getting the entire S&P 500 index: 500 of the most profitable large-cap stocks. When America's largest publicly traded companies do well, so will you.

This index changes day by day as some stocks go up and others go down, weighted by market cap. For example, if one company has a bad month, the S&P 500 index will hold a slightly lower amount of its stock than it did a month ago. If another company performs better, its stock will account for a larger percentage of the S&P 500 by weight.

This means that the S&P 500 index is constantly sorting the "winners" and "losers" for you. This doesn't mean that the S&P 500 index always goes up or is guaranteed to deliver positive returns. But because S&P 500 ETFs like VOO hold 500 stocks, they are broadly diversified. That tends to make their performance less volatile over the long run than that of individual stocks or sector-specific ETFs.

In the past five years, the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF has delivered annualized returns of about 12%.

But if you're worried that the recent stock market correction will mean lower returns in the future, keep this in mind: over the long run, since 1928, the S&P 500 has delivered annualized returns of 9.98%. That's lower than the past five years, but still a strong return.

Let's assume that VOO can continue delivering 9.98% annualized returns over the coming years -- even if there are bear markets along the way. And let's assume that someone starts investing $200 a month at age 30 and keeps investing for 37 years until their Social Security retirement age of 67.

If you invested $200 per month in VOO and earned 9.98% average annual return of 9.98%, after 10 years, your investments would grow to $38,213. After 20 years, you'd have $137,148. After 30 years, you'd have $393,294. And after 37 years, your investments would be worth $788,199.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 777%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 199% for the S&P 500.

#2928. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2926. Prediction: This Will Be Anthropic’s Stock Price by the End of 2026

Anthropic is getting caught up in the Nvidia sell-off.

By Keithen Drury – Nov 27, 2026 at 2:03AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

Anthropic’s (ANT +1.08%) first two days as a publicly traded company were already days for the history books. It started off trading at about $110 per share before closing at $97. That's still a double-digit percentage gain from its list price, so it was a great day. Then it was caught up in a rout of AI stocks and, on Nov. 24, fell below its IPO price.

With a tumultuous start, Anthropic is an intriguing stock, but is it a buy? Here's where I think Anthropic could end the year priced, and investors may want to pay attention to get maximum returns.

There are multiple ways to engage in the AI investing trend. You could invest in a hardware supplier like Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%) or Broadcom (AVGO -0.07%), or in a utility company benefiting from increased electricity usage. There are also construction companies and AI hyperscalers that are putting up hundreds of billions of dollars' worth of data center infrastructure.

However, all these styles of investments have one thing in common: None of them are 100% pure-play AI investments. These companies all have other business units that aren't exposed to AI. There are very few AI-focused stocks on the market, as the entry costs to get into the AI game are quite high.

However, all that changed when Anthropic went public. Anthropic is one of the two primary AI labs (the other being OpenAI). So, with it going public first, it is drawing a lot of investor interest. Anthropic is the poster child for AI investing, as all of its business comes from the usage of its Claude family of AI models. The more AI is used, the better Anthropic does. So, if the market is bullish on AI, Anthropic will do great. If it's bearish, the market will suffer.

Right now, it's pretty clear that the market has an overall bearish stance on the AI build-out. However, that's mostly the market making its opinion on massive infrastructure spending known. There isn't much that will stop the AI hyperscalers from investing in data centers, as they see a massive opportunity.

Anthropic is a bit different from most AI companies, as it doesn't own any computing assets. Instead, it chooses to rent them. So, the risk exposure from overbuilding is practically nothing, since it only rents computing power rather than owning it. That should help it be analyzed from a pure AI usage standpoint by the market, but if market sentiment is going one way, nothing will be able to save Anthropic from further downturn.

So, what's in store for the rest of 2026?

The reality is that the AI sector is being harmed by one company: Nvidia. The resignation and return of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang have shaken the AI industry, but aside from Nvidia, there really aren't many domino effects. Anthropic will be just fine.

I think the market will come to this realization over the next few weeks, and Anthropic stock will be bought up in December so that portfolio managers can show their clients they bought the hot new AI stock. This could contribute to Anthropic rising in a Santa Claus rally – the name of the rally that occurs during December due to heavy buying.

As a result, I wouldn't be surprised if Anthropic's stock returned to the $ 110-per-share level it opened at on Nov. 19.

On the flip side, if the market says bearish on AI, there could be more downside from here. However, I think the odds of that are lower, so I'm confident in saying Anthropic has ample upside from here.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 777%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 199% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

#2918. Rapid Recap: Nets 104, Bucks 97

The Milwaukee Bucks follow a string of solid games with one of their worst in a long time, losing at home to the Brooklyn Nets, 104-97. Tyler Herro led the Bucks with 28 points and five assists, while Kel'el Ware led the Bucks bench with 13 points and 14 rebounds. Julius Randle led the Nets with 28 points, and Noah Clowney led their bench with 17 points.

The game started with the Bucks on the attack, forcing three personal fouls in the first 90 seconds and exploiting the mismatches created when four forwards and one guard are the opposing starting five. Myles Turner wasted no time pulling out his three-ball from Toronto as the Bucks quickly built a 9-2 lead. But of course, the Nets started finding their shot as the Bucks let their feet off the gas, going on a 16-2 run as Milwaukee's offence dried up like the Sahara Desert. The Bucks didn't score another field goal until the 4:08 mark, when Jaime Jaquez Jr. finally buried a three, which set off an 8-2 run that included a sweet baseline dunk by Kel'el Ware. Despite being in the bonus for more than eight minutes, the Bucks couldn't take advantage, shooting only six free throws for the quarter and ending the period down 28-24.

The second quarter started where the first quarter ended: with Ryan Rollins throwing up ill-advised shots—he was bad in this one. Meanwhile, the Nets steadily built a 41-28 lead through the first five minutes. Thankfully, the young Bucks off the bench played their typical brand of high-energy defense and held Brooklyn to six points over the next five minutes, but unfortunately, the home side couldn't buy a bucket themselves. Milwaukee did at least outscore Brooklyn 11-4 in the final few minutes to cut the deficit to four, with Ware giving fans a reason to get hyped with a monster dunk before Jaquez scored a quick lay-up to end the half on a high note. Milwaukee headed into the locker room down 47-51.

Coming out of halftime, the Bucks kept up the pace and quickly regained the lead, largely thanks to 11 quick points from Herro, and it finally felt like the home team had found a flow. Unfortunately, that flow ended as Rollins decided to dominate the ball, force shots, and make head-scratching decisions in general. Eventually, the Nets crept back ahead after another scoring drought by the Bucks late in the third quarter. If not for Ware imposing his will in the paint, the Nets may have run away with it. Instead, the big man finished the third quarter with 10 points and 10 rebounds, scoring the final four to keep the deficit at six, 77-71.

The Nets extended their lead to 10 pretty quickly to open the final frame, as the Bucks struggled to get paint touches, something that seemed like it would come easier after getting in the paint so effectively against Philadelphia and Toronto. Milwaukee made a few pushes, but each one ended in an unforced error; they just couldn't get out of their own way. The game ended ugly as the Bucks turned it over nine times in the fourth quarter alone to give this one away. One of the more disappointing efforts of the last few years, frankly, and that's saying something.

26: the number of free throw attempts the Nets had, which allowed them to hang in the game despite their horrid shooting.

Ryan Rollins needs to be glued to the bench for a game (or five).

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2910. Nets snap a five game losing streak, beat the Bucks 104-97

The Brooklyn Nets and Milwaukee Bucks came into tonight's game like two school kids trotting onto the playground, one pointing and chirping at the other "you're the worst defense," while the other bickered back "no, you're the worst defense."

Each youngster had some merit to their argument. Brooklyn had just given up 130+ in three of their last four games, and ranked dead last in defensive rating ahead of the contest. Milwaukee ranked behind them by a few fractions of a point.

Brooklyn would eventually win the squabble, and the game, but it'd take them some time to get there, not that anyone's complaining here.

Brooklyn came out the gates without a whole lot of ball pressure. In fairness, they had Danny Wolf matched up initially against Tyler Herro, which leaves you very little spatial insurance to work with. However, the Nets also looked out of sync rotating around the Milwaukee offense, which didn't help either. As Brooklyn shot 1-6 from the field and the Bucks pranced out to a 9-2 lead, it just looked like more of the same in the early going.

As I mentioned, we'd eventually find out otherwise. And while real turnaround would soon come on the defensive end, things had to start for Brooklyn at the other.

After Milwaukee's opening jolt, the Nets went on a 13-0 run in the heart of the first to go up 22-12. Michael Porter Jr., who spoke to Brain Lewis this week about how he's struggled to "find his rhythm," was in his grove amid the stretch, putting in eight first period points.

Getting five from Day'Ron Sharpe and Julius Randle each, as well as eight points off the bench in the period, the Nets led after one by a 28-24 margin.

Despite being less of a scorer this time around, Mikel Brown Jr. looked comfortable out there early on as well, starting for a second straight game. He shot 0-5 from the field in the first half but clocked in as a +4 with two assists with zero turnovers.

Also like last game, Grant Nelson joined him on the floor for a few minutes in the second. Nelson only finished with one point, but grabbed six rebounds, contributing to a strong collective effort from the team on the glass, where the Nets won 52-44. Porter Jr. also grabbed 11 rebounds, his most in a game all season if you discount the overtime affair vs Boston.

Brooklyn would need every one of those boards especially in the first half, where neither team shot it at a particularly high clip, as the Nets posted .400/.348 splits, while the Bucks had .432/.273 marks.

At the break, Brooklyn led 51-47, largely thanks to Randle, who had 16 points on 7-10 shooting from the field and 2-4 from deep. MPJ went quiet in the second, still rocking with eight points by then. Day'Ron Sharpe put us on early triple-double watch with five points, 10 rebounds, and four assists at the break.

Herro, however, had all the stats to begin the third. He poured in nine points during the period's first four minutes to put his team back in the lead for the first time since the 6:57 mark of the first. The Nets, meanwhile, opened the period 3-12 from the field and 0-5 from deep. Two of the makes came from Brown Jr, so at least there's that.

Sarcasm aside, as one young guard for Brooklyn started to turn it on, another followed. After Milwaukee looked ready to pull away, Egor Dëmin checked in, scoring five points himself and assisting on two baskets in just a minute and a half. His effort helped ignited a 13-2 run that put the lead back in Brooklyn's hands.

At the same time, Randle continued to mine inside for points. He'd eventually go for 28 in the game on 10-16 shooting. That not only gave Brooklyn consistent offense, but slowed things down and allowed them to set up easily at the other end. Even with Herro's early offensive excellence, the Bucks went 7-23 from the field in the quarter, and the Nets led going into the fourth by a 77-71 score.

"I think everybody was able to just put their minds together and not just focus on team defense, but their own island, like how we say 'guard your yard,'" Dëmin said. "I think that's where it came from. Everything else was easier because everybody was so locked in and super focused on how to guard the man in front of you rather than relying on someone else around."

And believe it or not, in that period, the defense continued to improve. On Milwaukee's second possession of the frame, Porter Jr. poked the a ball away in the backcourt, which Nelson dove across mid court to save. Still, the Bucks got it up and forward, but then Keon Ellis came charging over, intercepting the rock and then dishing it ahead Porter Jr. for an and-one finish. That was just one of the Nets seven steals in the fourth.

"Go out there and play with physicality," Sharpe said. "Go out there and play with ball pressure. Execute with staples and have each others back. I felt like we did a great job at that tonight."

Sharpe never got the triple double, but did finish 11 points, five assists, and 14 rebounds while shooting 5-10 from the field and hitting a three.

The Nets got up by 11 early in the fourth. After a few turnovers early in the frame, the Bucks cut it back down to eight, and we looked in store for a tight finish.

But in a game where Brooklyn's supposed fatal flaw had carried them, it was only right that Noah Clowney, who's struggled with his shot all year, iced things out. In the fourth, he added nine points on 4-6 shooting, scoring from all over the court to keep the lead afloat. He finished with a season-high 17 points, also grabbing five rebounds while shooting 7-13 from the field.

"This is the Noah Clowney that we need," Fernández said. "It gives us a starter off the bench and it's very, very good for this group, because it brings a player on both ends."

Dëmin, who's also had a difficult start to the year, leveraged his size to play some inspiring defense down the stretch as well, finishing as a +5 in the quarter. For the game, he had 15 points, four assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block while shooting 5-11 from the field.

"Yeah, he went back to doing the simple things, and it started with his physicality on defense," Fernández said. "They felt him the whole game. His length. He can rebound, he can be in the gaps…Proud of him for sure."

Sure, the Nets still turned it over on back-t0-back possession with the Bucks only down 11 and under a minute to go. Dëmin missed also missed an open dunk. They're still a young, flawed team. They didn't solve all their issues tonight, but they did win the night, and snapped their five game losing streak.

Final: Brooklyn Nets 104, Milwaukee Bucks 97

Mo Wagner subbed out at the end of the first period and did not return for the rest of the game. At half, the Nets ruled him out with left knee soreness.

"I don't know. I was about to sub him in, and I saw him limping, and I was like I got to save him from himself," Fernández said. "He was there to do whatever it took for his team and his teammates, and at the end I decided to not bring him back in. Obviously, I don't know what it is, but the good thing, the great thing is he was cheering for everybody. He was cheering fro Grant [Nelson] the whole time, and that means a lot for the group, and it speaks to the type of person he is."

We'll provide updates on him once they're made available.

Day'Ron Sharpe had a season-high 14 rebounds tonight and also recorded his 5th double-double of the season with 11 points. It matches his double-double total from last season, tied as the 2nd most in his career (6, 2023-24). This was his 7th career game with 10+ PTS, 10+ REB and 5+ AST.

Noah Clowney had a season-high 17 points off the bench tonight against the Bucks. He has tied his season high with three 3-pointers made.

Michael Porter Jr. logged his third double-double of the season with 12 points and 11 rebounds. It is his 15th time recording at least 10 rebounds as a Net.

Brooklyn gets right back in action after the holiday with a road matchup against the Toronto Raptors on Friday. The North has lost seven of their last eight, including a contest to the Bucks on Monday. They're certainly looking at this one as a "get right" game. The game tips off at 7:30 p.m. EST.

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2905. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

The full list of holdings and current market values is available from CNBC.com's Berkshire Hathaway Portfolio Tracker.

Also, Buffett's annual letters to shareholders are highly recommended reading. There are collected here on Berkshire's website.

#2901. Diving deeper into Cavs 121-102 loss to Knicks: Even when the stars are at their best, this team isn’t

The Cleveland Cavaliers looked like they could compete with the New York Knicks until they couldn't. Cleveland went into the final quarter with a narrow 85-82 advantage, but then lost the fourth 39-17 to drop this game 121-102.

19 points on 9-of-15 shooting, 8 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 block

Bridges covered up for pretty pedestrian performances from Jalen Brunson and Karl-Anthony Towns. Bridges provided efficient scoring, was an impactful rebounder, and was good defensively. This all added up to the Knicks winning his minutes by 33.

30 points on 12-of-24 shooting, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 steals, 2 blocks

Mitchell was unstoppable in the first half. His 14 points in the opening quarter and six more in the second put the Cavs in a good position to steal this game. Mitchell seemingly couldn't miss before the break, going 8-for-11 from the field with most of his damage coming at the rim.

The magic went away in the second half. Mitchell scored just 10 points on 4-of-13 shooting in the final two quarters. Cleveland then proceeded to lose those minutes by 15.

The Cavs needed a special performance from Mitchell to defeat the defending champions. They had that in the first half, but not the second.

15 points on 6-of-14 shooting, 12 assists, 7 rebounds, 3 steals, 2 blocks

Hart is a winning player. He does all the little things that championship-level teams need from their supporting cast. Rebounding, additional ball handling, defensive versatility, and ancillary playmaking are attributes you hope to accumulate in your entire supporting cast. Hart provides that as just one player.

Those skills were all on display against the Cavs. Hart rebounded well, forced turnovers, and was a useful facilitator. The Cavs have often decided to cheat off Hart offensively. He didn't make them pay as a shooter, but he used the extra space to find openings for his teammates.

On a night when both teams got similar contributions from their stars, it was the play of the supporting players, like Hart and Jose Alverado (21 points on 8-of-10 shooting), that made all the difference.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Browns throwback shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cleveland gear HERE.

5 points on 2-of-8 shooting, 1 rebound, 0 assists

Merrill has had a tough time finding his shot this season. There's many things Merrill does well, but those are mostly muted if the outside shot isn't falling, as was once again the case here.

Head coach Kenny Atkinson has asked a lot out of Merrill this season. He's been consistently forced to guard up a spot or two when he's on the court — particularly when he's been inserted into the starting lineup. That isn't an ideal place for him considering he's undersized for that role, and has been sharing the floor with guards that don't pull their weight defensively.

I don't envy Merrill. He's been continually put into positions to fail this season.

10 points on 5-of-7 shooting, 5 rebounds, 1 assist

Allen is supposed to be a key member of this team, but you wouldn't necessarily know that from watching games like this. The Cavs need Allen to be a force inside as a roller and rebounder with Evan Mobley out of the lineup. He wasn't either of those things, and played with little to no energy, which is why the Cavs lost his minutes by 32.

The Cavs are built around their guards. If that backcourt is going to work on both ends, they need their interior players to consistently show up. They haven't, which is one of the many reasons for the underwhelming start to the season.

The Cavs benefited greatly from Bryant's energy and willingness to be physical. Both of those things stood out on a team that is in desperate need of interior size and presence. It's no coincidence the Cavs played their best basketball of the evening when he was on the court.

28 points on 7-of-19 shooting, 5 assists, 1 rebound

This was a solid night from Harden. He kept the offense moving, took smart shots, and continually forced his way to the line (13-for-14). A poor shooting night from three (1-for-8) kept this from being a great offensive game.

Defensively, the Cavs had some success with hiding him on Towns. Harden held his own in that matchup, resulting in the defense not completely falling apart with him on the floor.

Harden did a good job of forcing his way inside. The offense is in a much better place when he can consistently do that.

Jose Alverado scored as many points off the bench (21) as all five of Cleveland's reserves combined.

This was a bad night from Cleveland's supporting cast, particularly their bench. Tyrese Proctor had just two points on 1-of-6 shooting, Jaylon Tyson went 2-for-8 from the field with four turnovers, and Mario Hezonja registered no points in 10 minutes.

Conversely, New York got key contributions from their role players, including Alverado. He scored 16 of his 21 points in the fourth to help put the Cavs away.

This team makes much more sense on paper than it does on the court. The Cavs have more well-rounded role players than they have had in recent years. This has shown through when they get opportunities to shine. Yet, that may not be what works with this particular core.

Typically, the Cavs are at their best when Mitchell and Harden are playing well, as they were on Wednesday. This formula has been the recipe for their top performances, but the Cavs didn't come close to pulling off the upset because they got next to no help from their supporting cast. No player besides the starting backcourt scored more than 10 points. It's nearly impossible to win games against an elite opponent when your role players add nothing.

Somehow, the Cavs have constructed a team that relies heavily on their guards to be at their best to win alongside a supporting cast that doesn't really fit with how the guards play. That is a problem, and how you get nights like this where Peyton Watson and Mario Hezonja combine to take just five shots from the field.

The Cavs are pot committed to this backcourt both in terms of their recent contract extensions and how they play. Atkinson hasn't indicated that he wants to move away from this isolation- and high pick-and-roll-heavy offense that caters specifically to the two guards. But that style isn't conducive to a team that has struggled to knock down catch-and-shoot threes (36.3%, 16th in the league), doesn't have a versatile defense, and features two bigs that need to be force-fed to be effective offensively.

Koby Altman has built an interesting supporting cast. On paper, it would fit nicely with a team that played like the 2024-25 Cavs. However, this isn't that team. Any hopes of playing that way went out the window when they traded for Harden last winter.

Cleveland has been one of the most inconsistent groups this season. Few teams have as impressive highs and as disappointing lows. Many factors contribute to that, but one of them is that the puzzle pieces don't really fit together. Or at the very least, they don't fit with how they're being used now.

There's no easy fix for this. Getting a team to drastically change how they play 19 games into the season is nearly impossible. At the same time, there isn't an easy way to pivot off either the star or supporting players due to their contracts. It's difficult to see a trade drastically changing the outlook of this team. They're more or less committed to this current group and playstyle. And I'm just not sure what the pathway is to being a legit title contender, which is what you would hope they'd be coming off a run to the Eastern Conference Finals.

Even when the stars are at or near their best, this team isn't.

#2895. Dillon Brooks gets ejected with 23 seconds left vs. Rockets after getting into it with Kevin Durant

Phoenix Suns guard Dillon Brooks and Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant exchanged words at the end of Houston's 116-104 win on Wednesday night.

Durant was chippy all night, making sure to deliver extra trash talk against his former team.

With less than 30 seconds left in the game, the Rockets led by 10. In a wild moment, Durant and Brooks weren't really playing; they were just talking to each other near halfcourt while the action continued.

Houston's Fred VanVleet hit a jumper with 23.8 seconds left to make it a 114-102 game in the Rockets' favor. Brooks and Durant were chirping at each other and got in each other's faces.

The referees assessed each player a technical foul. Because Brooks had received a technical foul earlier in the quarter with 4:32 left, he was ejected. VanVleet drained a three and motioned to Brooks; they chirped and were both assessed technicals.

Durant finished the game with 20 points and 10 rebounds. With 46.3 seconds left in the second quarter, the veteran forward drained a 3-pointer. That basket helped him become the fifth player in NBA history to score at least 33,000 points. He joins LeBron James (43,672 and counting), Kareem Abdul-Jabbar (38,387), Karl Malone (36,298) and Kobe Bryant (33,643).

The win moved Houston to 14-5. The Rockets return to action Saturday, hosting the Philadelphia 76ers.

The loss dropped Phoenix to 9-8. The Suns had won four straight before Wednesday's loss, and now turn their attention to taking on the Dallas Mavericks on Friday night.

#2890. Game Recap: Suns run out of fuel in 116-104 loss to Rockets

The Phoenix Suns fought valiantly, but couldn't finish the road trip undefeated as the Houston Rockets held off a late push by the Suns and earned a 116-104 victory. Phoenix clawed within two at 99-97, but the Rockets finished on a 17-7 run.

Alperen Sengun finished the game with 23 points, 11 rebounds and 11 assists, recording his third triple-double of the season. Seven of those rebounds were on the offensive glass. Steven Adams also snatched six offensive boards, and the Rockets won the overall rebounding battle 51-38.

Oso Ighodaro had 13 points on 6-for-8 shooting, but managed just two rebounds in 20 minutes before fouling out. Khaman Maluach added 12 points of his own and made several outstanding plays, but is still a little too raw to compete with the burly Adams and All-Star Sengun.

The Suns were an abysmal 5-for-32 from three-point range in the game. Jordan Goodwin was 2-for-4, which made the rest of the team 3-for-28. That looks like a typo. Posting a -13 on the boards and shooting 15.6% from the three-point range makes it hard to win a basketball game. A bad shooting night is one thing, but the rebounding situation is starting to look like a fatal flaw for this squad.

Miles Bridges struggled through his worst offensive game of the season, recording just two points while shooting 0-6 from three-point range and 1-8 overall. Devin Booker had a game-high 25 points and Jalen Green chipped in 16, but they were 6-18 and 8-21 from the field, respectively. Booker did knock down 11-of-12 free throws to continue his impressive performance from the line this season.

The Suns' bench group played well overall. In addition to Maluach's performance, Goodwin scored 11 points and grabbed five rebounds, but played just 17 minutes. Kevin Durant recorded a double-double with 20 points and 10 rebounds, while Amen Thompson recorded his own with 18 and 10. That is two more than the Suns had.

The Rockets' starters were just too much for Phoenix to contend with tonight. Plain and simple.

The Suns' aspirations of advancing to the next round in the NBA Cup have been quashed with this loss.

Phoenix will try to get back in the win column when they host the Dallas Mavericks and Cooper Flagg at 7:00pm Friday.

The Rockets raced out to an 11-4 lead with Kevin Durant getting five quick points. The Suns recovered to tie it up at 11, and for a long stretch in the quarter it looked like we might be watching a defense-optional game.

KD kept pouring it in and already had 11 points when the Rockets went up 22-18. At that time, the Rockets held a 10-2 free-throw disparity over the Suns.

The Suns were trying to play physical, but the referees didn't let them impose their will, opting to let Durant and Adams flop to get calls. Maluach ended up with a fairly weak flagrant one as he somehow managed to plant Adams face first on the ground. Or maybe Adams just laid down that way on purpose.

Jordan Goodwin entered and hit a quick three-pointer to pull the Suns within one at 23-22. Goodwin is shooting a career-high 38.6% this season from three-point range.

A dunk and damage for a three-point play from Maluach tied the game at 25. Khaman was playing very well overall but still displayed hand problems during the game. Goodwin hit his second three-pointer to give the Suns a 32-27 lead courtesy of a 10-2 run. The first quarter ended 33-29 in the Suns' favor. Booker finished the quarter with 13 points and survived a massive block by Amen Thompson.

Goodwin was fouled on a three-point attempt after making his first two. He sank all three free throws to give the Suns their biggest lead of the game at 38-31. The Oso/Kennard/Gillespie/Brooks/Goodwin lineup meshed well together, but the Rockets started punishing them inside.

Steven Adams grabbed four quick offensive rebounds. There just isn't a player on the Suns with the size and strength of Adams.

He helped the Rockets go on a 13-7 run to cut the Suns' lead to 45-44. Maluach was still making his presence felt. A few plays after a block on Sengun, he nailed a jumper as the shot clock expired. In between that, he mishandled the ball on offense for a turnover. Such are the highs and lows with the 20-year-old.

Sengun was still winning the matchup overall. After another offensive rebound, Sengun hit a three to cap an 11-2 run that put Houston up 57-51. That turned into a 20-7 run by the time the quarter was over, and Houston led 66-56 at the half. The Suns had squandered their early lead and would trail the remainder of the game.

Booker finished the first half with 15 points and four assists, while Goodwin chipped in nine points.

The Rockets starters had a well-balanced attack. Durant scored 14, Sengun had 13 along with seven rebounds and six assists, Jabari Smith Jr. poured in 12, and Amen just missed double figures with nine points. Houston led the battle of the boards 27-15, including 12-4 on the offensive end. The Rockets shot 26 free throws, but made just 14, while the Suns were 11-14 from the charity stripe.

Sengun, Durant and Amen Thompson were back to mangling the Suns as Sengun inched closer to a triple-double. The Rockets pushed their lead to a game-high 14 as they took a 79-65 lead. Phoenix fought back to cut it to six at 79-73, but a three-pointer by Tari Eason pushed it right back to 12.

The Suns were 0-6 from three-point range in the third. They hung around but couldn't get closer than six points. Lapses like the shot clock violation on their last possession in the quarter seemed to pop up and prevent them from completely catching up.

Maluach and Goodwin finished the quarter with 11 points each. Houston led 92-81.

Luke Kennard was awarded a technical free throw to start the 4th quarter on a positive note. Oso followed that up with a layup and was fouled but missed the free throw. Then it was Gillespie with the bump and the bucket, but he converted his three-point play. Suddenly it was 92-87, and the Suns were right back in it.

The teams traded a few buckets before Kennard cut the lead to four at 99-95. Green blew past a defender for a layup. 99-97. VanVleet drained a dagger three to make it 104-98 after Maluach went 1-for-2 from the line.

Sengun dished out an assist with 3:49 left to notch his third triple-double of the season. Booker made a layup to help the Suns keep pace at 106-100, but after a defensive stop both Booker and Brooks missed wide-open looks. Jabari Smith Jr. missed the second of two free throws, but Sengun scored off another offensive rebound to push the lead to 109-100. A VanVleet three-pointer made it 112-102.

The game ended with Brooks and KD in each other's face. Dillon got to start his shower 23.8 seconds early. I was frustrated too. The Rockets held on for a 116-104 victory, evening the season series 1-1.

It's Dallas on Friday. We'll see you then!

#2889. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

#2888. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2884. San Antonio at Portland, Final Score: the Spurs lose their first road game 116-111 to the Blazers

The Spurs hadn't lost a road game yet this season, but that came to an end tonight as Damian Lillard with 29 points and Deni Avidja with 33 rallied Rip City to a 5 point win in a game that was a back and forth contest all night. Former Spur Sidy Cissoko had 16 points and made some key plays down the stretch as Victor Wembanyama's 44 points weren't enough to get the Silver and Black to a win. The Spurs shot poorly from three, but the bigger problem was lack of attention to detail, as some critical mistakes and missed free throws down the stretch were the deciding factor, as the Blazers thrilled the home crowd and pulled out a big NBA Cup win. The Spurs will have to win their game on Friday to have a shot at advancing the the knockout rounds in the Cup tournament. They have a pretty solid point differential, so even if they don't win the group, they could make it into the tournament as a wildcard. But first they have to win on Friday. Go Spurs, BEAT LA!

The lead-in game was Thunder vs Timberwolves, and the Wolves won 112-106, but mostly I was distracted by Chet Holmgren wearing a fur coat on the bench.

Dame was pretty much held in control in the first meeting, but tonight, he started out hot, scoring the first 7 for the Blazers. When he hit a logo triple at about four minutes in, Mitch had to call a quick TO. Wemby sat after the TO, taking a very short first rotation of just 4 minutes.

The Spurs bench did a decent job of holding back the Blazers until Wemby returned to the lineup with 3 minutes left in the quarter.

The Spurs attempted 15 free throws in the first quarter to 10 for Portland, which kept them in the game, as they trailed 35-38 after the first. The bench squad held up pretty well, with Jordan McLaughlin having some good minutes showing his quickness and ability to get to the rim.

Ja'Kobi Gillespie got into the game at the start of the second quarter, which is a good sign that he's already gaining Mitch Johnson's trust as a viable backup.

Advija is pretty adept at using his off arm to get around defenders. It's an offensive foul, but I guess if nobody is going to call it, it's a good move.

In their first meeting on November 7, the Spurs held Deni Avdija to 15 points. He scored 18 in the first half tonight. They held Lillard to 12, and he had an 18 point first half. This was going to be a tougher go for the Silver and Black, but they only trailed by three at the half, with the score at 60 Blazers, 57 Spurs.

The Blazers tried to single cover Wembanyama with Clingan. It didn't work out well for them.

How many time have we seen Wembanyama turn an ankle, and somehow be just fine after going to the floor for a minute or two. His physical resilience is superhuman.

The Spurs kept pace for most of the third quarter but a stretch of wacky ball about two thirds through the frame let the Blazers get some easy buckets as they took an 82-77 lead. Mitch called a timeout, and the Spurs put Wemby back in the game leading to a 7-0 Spurs run as they retook the lead. After a couple of Dame free throws, a Devin Vassell buzzer beater at the end of the quarter gave the Spur an 87-84 lead going into the fourth.

The Blazers had an offensive explosion to start the fourth quarter, with Dame hitting a deep three over Wembanyama and Sidy Cissoko making an impressive dunk on a drive to the bucket, and the home team was suddenly up 96-89, their biggest lead of the game.

Victor can't play the whole game, and the bench had to take a turn, but they actually held their own, and by the time that Wemanyama came in at 5:43, the Blazer lead was only 3 points, the bench had done their part, and it was Wemby's job to bring it home.

Clingan fouled out with 4 minutes and earned a tech for complaining about it. Sadly, the Silver and Black could not cash the free throw, and with a lane violation of one of Wemby's free throws, they left two points wanting, which could be costly.

The Blazers caused an 8 second call on Castle bringing up the ball, and after a few empty trips, a crazy banking three point shot from Avdija gave Portland a 5 point lead with under a minute left. After another empty trip by the Silver and Black, the Blazers had the ball and called timeout. They just had to get the ball in bounds and they had it in the bag, unless they turn it over on the inbounds play, which they did. The Spurs cut the lead to one score with 17 seconds left, but the Blazers were done giving gifts to the Spurs, and they closed it out with free throws, winning 116-111.

For the opponents' viewpoint, visit Blazer's Edge.

The Spurs return home on Friday night to face the Lakers with a chance advance to the knockout rounds of the Emirates Cup, and earn an extra payday for the players, along with getting more experience with being in a playoff-like atmosphere.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

#2879. This Anthropic proxy play is getting traders' attention – here's where they see it going

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Since then, the fund has barely moved, trading around $26.There are other bullish signs surrounding Anthropic's IPO in the derivatives markets. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.

#2878. Rockets Scurry Over Suns To Another 116-104 Victory

Another game, another 12pt victory that never felt comfortable, but never seemed much in doubt, either. While the previous opponent, the LA Clippers are not a good team, the Suns are good, or at least decent. They beat the Rockets in Phoenix not that long ago, in a game that has been described as strange, or one where the Rockets seemed asleep.

Because I've been dealing with an household electrical problem (that took out the refrigerator) which, of course, cropped up on Thanksgiving Eve; this might be a shorter recap. (I fixed the problem BTW, Thanksgiving is saved, hooray for me.) It's also a shorter recap because it's hard to pin much of a narrative on the past two Rockets game. They look like easy wins, but weren't. The Rockets didn't play their best, but won. The defense didn't seem great, but was. No one player stood out as the star, or the goat, of the team. The offense didn't look great, but it was above NBA average.

In both of the games, Clippers and Suns, it seemed as though the Rockets knew they were more talented than their opponent, turned on the afterburners for a short time, and then just played keep away.

Here's what I mean – quarter by quarter scoring tonight:

The Rockets scored a lot for the first half, then just kind of added slightly to a lead that spent the time from around the middle of the second quarter onward fluctuating between about 10 to 6 points.

The Suns obviously came out in this game with a plan to play physically with the Rockets. This lead to a whopping 26 free throws in the first half for the Rockets. In troubling news, the Rockets made only 14 of those 26 free throws, or this could have been a substantially more comfortable win. Sengun went 2-6, Thompson 4-9, Jabari 3-5, though Durant was more consistent, at 5-6. Where the good free throw shooting from the earliest part of the season has gone is anyone's guess. It hasn't cost the Rockets as it did in Denver, but neither the Suns nor the Clippers are a strong a team as the Nuggets. If it doesn't improve, though, it might cost them as soon as Saturday, when the Rockets face the 15-4 Philadelphia 76ers.

The Suns changed tack in the second half, apparently, and the Rockets only shot six more free throws in the second half.

The Rockets continue not to have a single fulcrum in terms of scoring or usage, but it's clear the engine remains Alperen Sengun. Sengun is averaging 22pts/11rbs/7ast/1stl/1blk/3TO on 55%FG shooting, 63TS%, 50%3pt on 26% usage in 33mpg. If you asked most NBA fans who was playing better, Sengun or Luka Doncic, they'd all say Doncic. But Doncic is shooting worse overall at 46%, his True Shooting Percentage is lower, despite him taking over 10FT per game, his assists are about 3/4 of an assist higher, than Sengun, his rebounds 4 lower, and his usage is sitting at a staggering 41% compared to Sengun's 26%. The argument can be made that Doncic's gaudy points total is largely driven by usage, while Sengun is the more effective player. Yes, Doncic is a heliocentric player, so perhaps he gets less help (perhaps), but that's by design.

Anyhow, Sengun has been fantastic, those are excellent numbers, and his effect is similarly profound for the Rockets. He remains, despite everything, 24 1/3 years old. He's also making 50% of his threes.

At some point we will have to have a serious conversation about Ime Udoka's truest soulmate, Marcus Smart. He's an intense player, and an incredibly streaky shooter. He also can simply lose his mind in a game, and throw a punch, or beef with a fan, or any of a variety of undesirable things. Tonight he almost single-handedly lead a Phoenix comeback tonight by committing a shooting foul on three consecutive plays before he was thankfully removed. Were they all fouls? Pretty much, yes.

We also got a waning seconds, late game stare down standoff, between Dillon Brooks and Kevin Durant. It looked for an instant like Durant was contemplating throwing a punch. If he was Marcus Smart, he probably would have done it. Instead he settled for an unnecessary dunk with seconds remaining. This marked another good game for Durant with 20pts on 7-15 shooting but a woeful 1-7 from three. Extrapolating from that he was almost perfect with his mid range shot, and it looking exactly as great as 7-8.

The next highest scorer was Amen Thompson at 18 points, and it could have been better if he made more free throws. Amen has returned to form, and he had a lot of space to work with near the rim tonight as the Suns seemed extremely focused on Sengun.

The Rockets looked like they might give this one away in the fourth quarter, but the man the Rockets missed last season, Fred VanVleet did indeed ride to the rescue, steadied the ship, toed the line, and took the wheel. So for a second game things might look a bit better on the scoreboard than the Rockets played. At least their mental fragility didn't wreck them. Dillon Brooks would not have let them hear the end of it.

The Rockets and Suns overall played a pretty feisty, chippy game with a lot of the player chosen with the Rockets pick last year Khaman Maluach trying to molest Alperen Sengun in a basketball sense. This was not successful, but he's a powerful player with a nice close shooting touch who might one day be excellent. Today was not that day.

Tomorrow, though, is Thanksgiving! On behalf of TDS, happy Thanksgiving to all who celebrate, and our best wishes to those who don't.

#2877. Cowboys vs. Eagles recap: Dallas embarrassed by Philadelphia on Thanksgiving Day

It will go down in history as one of the darkest Thanksgiving Day games ever for the Dallas Cowboys.

Somehow, some way, the Cowboys threw away a 31-10 lead that they had in the third quarter.

The Philadelphia Eagles won on what was basically a game-winning, 60-yard field goal. It is hard to fathom.

On the other hand it is actually very easy to fathom given everything we have seen from the Cowboys this season. They are not a good football team. They are poorly coached. They are talent-deficient. They are all of the bad things.

On a day where we are supposed to be thankful it is hard to find a reason to be for this specific part of our lives. The Dallas Cowboys are a weekly embarrassment and Thursday was the latest example of it.

The Eagles won the toss and deferred which put the Cowboys offense to work first.

Dak Prescott and the group did not waste the opportunity. Javonte Williams was served seconds, thirds, and then some as the team marched down the field, partly thanks to a huge 3rd and 18 conversion from Dak to CeeDee Lamb, but it was Williams who capped it off with the touchdown to give the home team an early 7-0 lead.

Things were looking rather impressive for Joe Milton and the Eagles. I'd imagine that many Cowboys fans were starting to assume a score was happening any minute across the first drive.

Thankfully that was not the case. Right around field goal range the Eagles chose to roll the dice on fourth and short… and Quinnen Williams said no!

Weirdly a sequence occurred on the next drive that did the first.

Dak Prescott scrambled for a first on a third down, but the gain was negated by a penalty. This brought up third and long (16 here, 18 before) and Prescott connected with a star receiver (George Pickens here, CeeDee Lamb before) for the first down.

After that KaVontae Turpin had a stellar catch where he turned on the boosters and reached the endzone in a flash. Dallas was up 14-0 early.

The quarter ended with Philly holding the ball right after.

The Eagles punted shortly after teams flipped the field which put the Cowboys offense back up. Given that they were already leading 14-0 this was a chance to really blow things open. Unfortunately they could not and wound up punting the ball for the first time in a while.

A big 3rd and 12 conversion from Philadelphia put them within striking distance and while they were unable to fully get home they did give Jake Elliott a chance at a 48-yard field goal. It was good and the visitors had their first points on the day.

The Cowboys offense did not miss this time around. KaVontae Turpin was involved again, this time on a jet sweep, and there was a big conversion from Dak Prescott to CeeDee Lamb.

But it was Javonte Williams who cased in yet again. This marked his second score on the day and gave Dallas a 21-3 lead.

Joe Milton had been showing moments of promise to this point in the game and at long last he delivered. It was a classic Joe Milton throw… meaning it traveled quite a long ways.

The Cowboys received the ball with just over four minutes to go. It felt like an important possession as it would likely be their last one before the half and they received the ball to start the game. Points were essential.

In all honesty it was a pretty penalty-filled drive. Officials were calling them left and right, many of them helping the Cowboys, but Dallas was moved back after an apparent touchdown to Brevyn Spann-Ford.

On third and goal from the 12-yard line… Dak Prescott found Javonte Williams for a score to close the half. It also marked a hat trick of scores on the day for Williams specifically.

The Eagles took a knee and it was Miranda Lambert time.

Philadelphia went three and out to start the second half which is always great to see. The Cowboys took the field and once again with an opportunity to stretch their lead.

Dallas punted it back themselves. This game truly felt over, but obviously with a lot of time left that was not the case in any real way. Football had to be played.

Shemar James sacked Joe Milton which helped to ensure the punt streak continued. Philly booted it back and the Cowboys offense returned to the field.

It was a little bit of the same for the Cowboys offense in terms of not being able to fully convert. They did score, but only 3 points by way of Brandon Aubrey. This was notable though as it was a 59-yard field goal that he drilled with ease which was great to see given how this season has gone. The FOX broadcast also noted that this was the longest ever field goal made on Thanksgiving all-time. Pretty neat.

The third quarter ended right as Philadelphia crossed the 50-yard line… and then Joe Milton found DeVonta Smith again to get another touchdown on the board with 15 seconds left. It was suddenly 31-17, good guys.

Things did not go particularly well for the Cowboys offense to start the fourth quarter. They faced a fourth and short on their side of the field and left the offense on the field. It was bold, but nobody really thought they were going to run a play. Thankfully they just fished for offsides and punted.

Or so we thought. The Cowboys tried to punt… but it was blocked and returned for a Philly touchdown. Out of nowhere it was 31-24 with basically the entire fourth quarter left.

It goes without saying that the pressure was high. The Cowboys were on the verge of blowing this game. Something had to happen.

Dak Prescott and CeeDee Lamb specifically played like that was the case. They hooked up for a pair of big gains along the drive and then Dak put the exclamation point on things himself by running it on for a touchdown… thanks to some serious hops.

It seemed like this was going to essentially end the game. Have you seen the 2026 Dallas Cowboys, though

Philadelphia traveled 42 yards in 3 plays to match the touchdown. Joe Milton literally did by rushing it in himself.

The Eagles missed the two-point conversion so it was 38-30 Cowboys. In the blink of an eye it was 3rd and 14 right past the team's own 15-yard line.

It appeared as if the Cowboys had pulled off something truly incredible. Dak Prescott found George Pickens for a modest gain, but then he threw a lateral across the field to Hunter Luepke and the fullback took it all the way to the house. He even jumped into the Salvation Army kettle.

Unfortunately it was deemed to be a forward pass from Pickens and therefore a penalty which resulted in the loss of the down. Bryan Anger successfully got the punt off this time and the Eagles returned it past midfield. There was still half the fourth quarter remaining.

It was then that the Cowboys defense made a play. On third and forever they picked off Joe Milton. Cobie Durant was the hero in question.

You would think that this would have given the Cowboys the opportunity to ice the game. You would think that the offense that had been on fire for so long would have scored to fully put things away.

They wound up settling for a field goal attempt from 40 yards out. Brandon Aubrey missed. It was unbelievable.

Fine. Mistakes happen. Surely the Cowboys defense would lock in.

It took the Eagles just 4 plays to travel 70 yards and score. Seriously.

Given the failed two-point conversion earlier though the Eagles had to have one here. They failed again so Dallas retained a slim lead at 38-36.

The Cowboys got past midfield. In fact, they got all the way to the Philadelphia 39-yard line.

They were facing 4th and 1. Instead of letting Brandon Aubrey try a field goal, he had just missed obviously, the Cowboys chose to go for it. Dak Prescott was sacked. It felt grim.

You know what happened. The Eagles pulled off a 60-yard field goal to win 39-38.

The Cowboys' season is over. Unbelievable.

#2875. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2871. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.

Federal Register notice: Appraisals for Higher-Priced Mortgage Loans Exemption Threshold (PDF)
